# Proposed OEIS comments: A260367 to A264623

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A260367

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A260367>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [4485](../papers/04001-04500/04485-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-3) +19*a(n-4) -18*a(n-6) -124*a(n-7) -163*a(n-8) +10*a(n-9) +252*a(n-10) +864*a(n-11) +879*a(n-12) -174*a(n-13) -1591*a(n-14) -3680*a(n-15) -3315*a(n-16) +1266*a(n-17) +6073*a(n-18) +10814*a(n-19) +9133*a(n-20) -5114*a(n-21) -15884*a(n-22) -23402*a(n-23) -19214*a(n-24) +13236*a(n-25) +30495*a(n-26) +38782*a(n-27) +32769*a(n-28) -23424*a(n-29) -45619*a(n-30) -49722*a(n-31) -47865*a(n-32) +30226*a(n-33) +55237*a(n-34) +48214*a(n-35) +60530*a(n-36) -30888*a(n-37) -53980*a(n-38) -34404*a(n-39) -61349*a(n-40) +24766*a(n-41) +39056*a(n-42) +18624*a(n-43) +46236*a(n-44) -13648*a(n-45) -19198*a(n-46) -7988*a(n-47) -24471*a(n-48) +4392*a(n-49) +6110*a(n-50) +2386*a(n-51) +8570*a(n-52) -662*a(n-53) -1089*a(n-54) -306*a(n-55) -1825*a(n-56) -6*a(n-57) +60*a(n-58) -24*a(n-59) +201*a(n-60) +8*a(n-61) +3*a(n-62) +6*a(n-63) -8*a(n-64) for n>66

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 384 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 66. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A260370

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A260370>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2721](../papers/02501-03000/02721-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A261681

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A261681>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11220](../papers/11001-11500/11220-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -(n+1)*(n-4)*a(n) +(3*n^2-9*n-8)*a(n-1) +2*(n^2-9*n+16)*a(n-2) +4*(-3*n^2+18*n-25)*a(n-3) +8*(n-3)^2*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 04 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. ```

## A262768

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A262768>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11326](../papers/11001-11500/11326-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -2*(n+2)*(49*n-95)*a(n) + 325*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n-1) + 2*(67*n-38)*(2*n-5)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 07 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A262920

Paper: [10922](../papers/10501-11000/10922-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 65 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 360 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 65, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A263053

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263053>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3747](../papers/03501-04000/03747-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A263119

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263119>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3219](../papers/03001-03500/03219-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-4) - 2*a(n-5) = A265381(n+2).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(14 + x + x^2 + x^3 - 14*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 15 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263459>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3011](../papers/03001-03500/03011-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + a(n-5) - 3*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - 3*x - x^2 - x^3 - 2*x^4 - 3*x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 + x + x^2 + x^3 + x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 11 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = (4^(n+1) + 3*2^((n-3)/2)*(4 + sqrt(2) + (-1)^n*(sqrt(2) - 4)))/7. - _Stefano Spezia_, Dec 26 2021

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A263552

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263552>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11053](../papers/11001-11500/11053-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 47 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 49 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 47, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264268

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264268>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [345](../papers/00001-00500/00345-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 52 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 52, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264507>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1053](../papers/01001-01500/01053-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 3*a(n-3) + a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5) - a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - x + 2*x^3 - x^4) / (1 - 2*x - x^2 + 3*x^3 - x^4 - 2*x^5 + x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 08 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264509

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264509>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [293](../papers/00001-00500/00293-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 69 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 69, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264572>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [334](../papers/00001-00500/00334-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
